//! Release activation extends the existing engine transaction and generation.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — CAS includes every activation, not only semantic changes.
use super::*;
use rusqlite::OptionalExtension;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct ReleaseActivation {
    pub release: u64,
    pub expected_previous: Option<u64>,
    pub expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    pub restore_dormant: bool,
    pub native_digest: Option<String>,
    pub operation: String,
}
impl ReleaseActivation {
    pub fn new(release: u64, expected_previous: Option<u64>) -> Self {
        Self {
            release,
            expected_previous,
            expected_revision: None,
            restore_dormant: true,
            native_digest: None,
            operation: "activate".into(),
        }
    }
    pub(crate) fn check(&self, conn: &rusqlite::Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let actual: Option<String> = conn
            .query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release'", [], |r| {
                r.get(0)
            })
            .optional()?;
        if actual
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok() != self.expected_previous)
        {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("current release changed; run `snapback4 releases <dir>` and retry the selected release".into()));
        }
        let revision: Option<String> = conn
            .query_row(
                "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release_revision'",
                [],
                |r| r.get(0),
            )
            .optional()?;
        let revision = revision.and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        if self.expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != revision) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("activation revision changed (including rollback/equal-content activation); run `snapback4 releases <dir>` and retry".into()));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl Engine {
    pub fn new_release(
        store: Store,
        schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        release: u64,
        expected_previous: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_release_with_feed_adoption(
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            release,
            expected_previous,
            false,
        )
    }
    pub fn new_release_with_feed_adoption(
        store: Store,
        schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        release: u64,
        expected_previous: Option<u64>,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_release_prepared(
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            &ReleaseActivation::new(release, expected_previous),
            allow_feed_adoption,
        )
    }
    pub fn new_release_prepared(
        store: Store, schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation, allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_release_prepared_at(store,schema,programs,options,allow_feed_adoption,crate::now_ms())
    }
    pub fn new_release_prepared_at(
        mut store: Store,
        mut schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
        now: i64,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        prepare(&mut schema)?;
        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(
            &mut store,
            &schema,
            &programs,
            Some(options),
            now,
            allow_feed_adoption,
            None,
            None,
        )?;
        store.exclude_ephemeral(&schema);
        Ok(Self {
            timeline_schedule: timeline::Schedule::default(),
            ephemeral: std::sync::Arc::new(ephemeral::Hub::new(
                metadata.store_id,
                generation,
                &schema,
            )),
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            generation,
            expiry_after: BTreeMap::new(),
            dev_effects: false,
            ceilings: Ceilings::default(),
            entropy: crate::ids::seed(),
            minter: crate::ids::Mint::default(),
            #[cfg(feature = "native")]
            native: None,
            #[cfg(feature = "native")]
            native_logs: native::Logs::default(),
            presence: None,
        })
    }
    /// No replacement engine, post-commit SQL read, or fallible ephemeral swap.
    pub fn adopt_release(
        &mut self, schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation, allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.adopt_release_at(schema,programs,options,allow_feed_adoption,crate::now_ms())
    }
    pub fn adopt_release_at(
        &mut self,
        mut schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
        now: i64,
    ) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        prepare(&mut schema)?;
        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
        drop(self.ephemeral.lock()?);
        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(
            &mut self.store,
            &schema,
            &programs,
            Some(options),
            now,
            allow_feed_adoption,
            None,
            None,
        )?;
        self.store.exclude_ephemeral(&schema);
        self.timeline_schedule = timeline::Schedule::default();
        self.schema = schema;
        self.programs = programs;
        self.generation = generation;
        self.expiry_after.clear();
        self.dev_effects = false;
        self.ephemeral
            .rebind_committed(metadata.store_id, generation, &self.schema);
        Ok(generation)
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "native")]
    pub fn set_prepared_native(&mut self, module: Option<std::sync::Arc<native::Module>>) {
        self.native = module;
    }
}

fn prepare(schema: &mut Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    columns::validate_before_prepare(schema)?;
    schema.check_row_view_audiences()?;
    schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
    schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
    if let Some(issue) = crate::schema::validate(schema).first() {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
            "{}: {}",
            issue.path, issue.message
        )));
    }
    Ok(())
}
//! Owner-mediated release publication in the running HTTP process.
use super::*;
use crate::release_control::Command;
#[cfg(unix)]
use crate::release_control::Listener;
use snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation;

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(super) fn start(
    shared: Arc<Shared>,
    dir: std::path::PathBuf,
) -> Result<std::thread::JoinHandle<()>, String> {
    let store = shared
        .owner
        .as_ref()
        .ok_or("release control requires a persisted owner")?
        .path()
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let listener = Listener::bind(&dir, store)?;
    crate::lifecycle::managed_cleanup();
    crate::lifecycle::background("release-control", move || {
        listener.run(|command| apply(&shared, &dir, command))
    })
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — only assignments and committed rebind follow the database commit.
pub(super) fn apply(
    shared: &Shared,
    dir: &std::path::Path,
    command: Command,
) -> Result<Json, String> {
    match command {
        Command::Status {
            after,
            items_after,
            event,
        } => {
            let mut engine=lock(&shared.engine);
            let mut status=engine.store_mut().release_status(after,items_after.as_deref(),event).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            status["clock"]=json!({"now":shared.now(),"offsetMs":lock(&shared.clock).offset()});
            Ok(status)
        },
        Command::Prune {
            confirm,
            expected_revision,
        } => {
            let mut result = lock(&shared.engine)
                .store_mut()
                .prune_dormant(confirm, Some(expected_revision))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if let Some(items) = result["items"].as_array_mut() {
                items.truncate(16);
            }
            result["items_after"] = result["items"]
                .as_array()
                .and_then(|items| items.last())
                .map(|i| i["key"].clone())
                .unwrap_or(Json::Null);
            Ok(result)
        }
        Command::Activate {
            release,
            expected_release,
            expected_revision,
            digest,
            rollback,
            restore_dormant,
            allow_feed_adoption,
        } => {
            if release == 0 {
                return Err("release id must be positive".into());
            }
            let project = Project {
                root: dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string()),
            };
            if !std::fs::symlink_metadata(&project.root)
                .is_ok_and(|m| m.is_dir() && !m.file_type().is_symlink())
            {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} is not an immutable release directory"
                ));
            }
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("staged release content changed; redeploy its exact source".into());
            }
            if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release"
                ));
            }
            let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
                return Err(bound
                    .diagnostics
                    .iter()
                    .map(ToString::to_string)
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join("\n"));
            }
            let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
            crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("release artifacts changed during preparation; retry after inspecting immutable source".into());
            }
            crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
            let mut pointer =
                crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, expected_release, rollback)?;
            let options = ReleaseActivation {
                release,
                expected_previous: expected_release,
                expected_revision: Some(expected_revision),
                restore_dormant,
                native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
                operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
            };
            let _requests=shared.release_requests.write();
            let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
            lock(&shared.readers).begin_adoption(&bound);
            let generation = match engine.adopt_release_at(
                bound.schema.clone(),
                bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()),
                &options,
                allow_feed_adoption,
                shared.now(),
            ) {
                Ok(generation) => generation,
                Err(error) => {
                    lock(&shared.readers).finish_adoption();
                    return Err(error.to_string());
                }
            };
            #[cfg(test)]
            test_work(&shared.release_committed);
            engine.set_prepared_native(prepared.module);
            engine.set_presence(Some(shared.presence.clone()));
            shared.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, shared.now());
            lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &bound.refusal_sites);
            {
                let mut active = lock(&shared.bound);
                let mut advertised = lock(&shared.generation);
                let mut native = lock(&shared.native);
                shared.latency.adopt(&bound);
                *active = bound;
                *advertised = generation;
                *native = prepared.installed;
                let mut root = lock(&shared.release_root);
                *root = Some(project.root);
                shared.worker_revision.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Release);
            }
            crate::lifecycle::generation(generation);
            shared.publish_engine(&engine);
            // Completion I/O can fail, but never abandons the new runtime.
            pointer.finish(generation)?;
            snapback4_core::diagnostic!(
                "snapback4 serve: release {release} activated without restart as generation {generation}"
            );
            Ok(
                json!({"release":release,"generation":generation,"revision":expected_revision+1,"hot":true,"adoption":engine.store().adoption_report.compact(),"details":engine.store().adoption_report.summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report.backfills}),
            )
        }
    }
}
//! Operator preflight runs the real activation on a private disk-backed snapshot.
// REL3 owns dormant-visibility counts here; SURF clause narration lives in release_narration.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — no live pointer, generation or audit changes.
use super::*;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Snapshot {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    }
}

pub(super) fn preview(
    store_path: &Path,
    root: PathBuf,
    release: u64,
    options: &Options,
) -> Result<(), String> {
    let bound = super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
    let project = Project { root };
    let native = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project,&bound.schema)?;
    let id = snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default())
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let snapshot = Snapshot(std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("snapback4-release-preview-{id}")));
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&snapshot.0).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let path = snapshot.0.join("store.sqlite");
    // SQLite VACUUM INTO takes a coherent read snapshot even with a live WAL writer.
    // Busy/disk errors refuse this preview; never fall back to copying database bytes.
    if store_path.exists() {
    let source = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    source
        .conn()
        .execute("VACUUM INTO ?1", [path.to_string_lossy().as_ref()])
        .map_err(|e| {
            format!("snapshot preview failed (retry after store/disk becomes available): {e}")
        })?;
    drop(source);
    }
    let mut store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let held = store
        .release_status(0, None, None)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — preview uses the owning host's authoritative time, without minting or advancing anything.
    let activation_now=if crate::store_owner::inspector_address(store_path).is_some() {
        let status=crate::release_control::request(store_path.parent().ok_or("preview store has no deployment directory")?,crate::release_control::Command::Status{after:0,items_after:None,event:None})?;
        if status["revision"]!=held["revision"] {return Err("preview raced release activation; retry against the current store".into());}
        status["clock"]["now"].as_i64().ok_or("serving owner does not expose its activation clock; restart it with this CLI before preview")?
    }else{snapback4_core::now_ms()};
    let mut activation =
        snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(release, held["release"].as_u64());
    activation.expected_revision = held["revision"].as_u64();
    activation.native_digest = native.installed.map(|n| n.digest);
    activation.operation = "preview".into();
    activation.restore_dormant = !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") || options.flags.contains_key("restore-dormant");
    let mut steps = Vec::new();
    let estimate = store
        .adoption_estimate(&bound.schema)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .map(|e| e.summary());
    let candidate_schema=bound.schema.clone();
    let candidate_programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|(p.program.name.clone(),p.program.clone())).collect();
    let mut hazards=Vec::new();
    let mut notes=Vec::new();
    let prior:Option<(String,String)>=store.conn().query_row("SELECT schema,programs FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?))).ok();
    let prior_schema=prior.as_ref().map(|(schema,_)|serde_json::from_str::<snapback4_core::Schema>(schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).transpose()?;
    if let Some((schema,programs))=prior {
        let prior:snapback4_core::Schema=serde_json::from_str(&schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        steps=snapback4_core::successor::classify(&prior,&candidate_schema);
        let programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::Program>=serde_json::from_str(&programs).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let target_label = format!("release {release}");
        let compatibility = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &prior, &candidate_schema, held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0), &target_label, options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
        for line in compatibility {
            if let Some(warning) = line.strip_prefix("WARNING ") { hazards.push(warning.to_owned()); }
            else { notes.push(line.trim_start_matches("note: ").to_owned()); }
        }
        let rollback=!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction");
        let (behavior_warnings, behavior_notes) = super::release_narration::behavior(store_path, &store, &prior, &candidate_schema, &programs, &candidate_programs, &project, held["release"].as_u64(), &target_label, rollback)?;
        hazards.extend(behavior_warnings); notes.extend(behavior_notes);
        for name in prior.tables.keys().filter(|name|!candidate_schema.tables.contains_key(*name) && prior.maintain_for(name).is_none() && prior.tables[*name].ephemeral.is_none()) {
            let count:u64=store.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1)",[name],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if count>0 {hazards.push(format!("table {name}: {count} rows become dormant; {target_label} lacks this table's behavior; preserve it in a bridge or accept its absence"));}
        }
    }
    super::release_safeguards::capture(&store)?;
    let mut engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared_at(
        store,
        bound.schema,
        bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        &activation,
        options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),
        activation_now,
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let mut planned = engine
        .store_mut()
        .release_status(0, None, None)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    planned["items"] = serde_json::to_value(
        engine
            .store_mut()
            .dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX)
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let items: Vec<snapback4_core::store::DormantItem> = serde_json::from_value(planned["items"].clone()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let mut effects = super::release_safeguards::warnings(engine.store(),prior_schema.as_ref(),&candidate_schema,&candidate_programs,&items,release,!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
    effects.append(&mut hazards); hazards=effects;
    for fill in &engine.store().adoption_report.backfills { notes.push(format!("backfill: {fill}; confirm these historical values before selecting release {release}")); }
    steps.retain(|step|!step.message.contains("engine capture"));
    for step in &mut steps {
        if step.change==snapback4_core::successor::Change::Narrowing {
            step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
            step.message=format!("{}; active rows satisfy the candidate after dormant projection",step.message.split(';').next().unwrap_or(&step.message));
        }
        if let Some(position)=step.path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|s|s.strip_suffix(']')).and_then(|s|s.parse::<usize>().ok()) {
            let label=candidate_schema.maintains[position].label();
            let visits=engine.store().adoption_report.views.iter().find(|view|view.name==label).map(|v|v.rows).unwrap_or(0);
            step.message=format!("maintain {label}: rebuilt from {visits} source rows");
            step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
        }
        let path=step.path.strip_prefix("tables.").unwrap_or(&step.path).replace(".columns.",".");
        let count=planned["items"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().find(|i|i["path"].as_str()==Some(path.as_str())).and_then(|i|i["rows"].as_u64()).unwrap_or(0);
        let unit = if step.path.contains(".columns.") { "cells" } else if step.path.contains(".indexes.") { "entries" } else { "rows" };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — per-path prose uses the same disjoint counts as the inventory totals.
        let retained = if count == 0 && unit == "cells" {
            format!("no standalone {unit} become dormant (cells of any dormant rows are counted with those rows)")
        } else if count == 0 {
            format!("no {unit} become dormant")
        } else {
            format!("its {unit} become dormant ({count} {unit}; restored on forward)")
        };
        step.message=step.message.replace("its rows are lost", &retained).replace("its values are lost", &retained);
    }
    let checklist = super::release_bridge::checklist(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release,&hazards);
    if let Some(path) = options.flags.get("bridge") {
        let current = held["release"].as_u64().ok_or("a bridge needs a current deployed release")?;
        super::release_bridge::write(Path::new(path),store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?,current,release,&checklist)?;
        eprintln!("bridge starter written to {path}; edit its current source and follow ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md before activation");
    }
    if options.json {
        println!("{}",serde_json::to_string_pretty(&serde_json::json!({"preview":true,"checklist":checklist,"hazards":hazards,"notes":notes,"current":held,"plan":planned,"steps":steps,"estimate":estimate,"phases":engine.store().adoption_report.summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report.backfills})).unwrap());
    } else {
        println!("preview: release {release} would be current as generation {}; live release unchanged", engine.generation());
        super::release_summary::print(prior_schema.as_ref(), &candidate_schema, &candidate_programs, &hazards, &notes, &steps, &items);
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") { println!("  checklist: prove the named refusal cases and affected lists/counts as real accounts before release {release}; `--bridge <new-dir>` saves current source plus this app's ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md"); }
        println!("  storage: {}",snapback4_core::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items));
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            for hazard in hazards { println!("  detail: WARNING {hazard}"); }
            for note in notes { println!("  detail: note: {note}"); }
            for step in steps {println!("  {}: {}",step.path,step.message);}
            for item in &items { println!("  {}: {} {} dormant (restored on compatible forward); sample ids [{}]", item.key, item.rows, item.unit(), item.sample_ids.join(", ")); }
            println!("{}", engine.store().adoption_report.summary());
            if let Some(estimate) = estimate {
                println!("{estimate}");
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
design revisions and implementation round 5 are READY from Muse and fresh
Codex. The checker preserves explicit unproved exposure paths and finite
counterexamples never become reachability proofs. The exposure map includes
inherited grants and their writers; new advice covers role/grant lifetimes,
grant arguments/global bootstrap, omitted evidence, sweep timing and refusal
oracles. The guide and all shipped todo/social/chat/production starter schemas
state their privacy intent. One new online-only guide mutation updates the
authoring census; the 94 private eligible sites stay unchanged.

Reports and all 150 schema hashes: `reports/20261018-sec/`. Main 20k and device
0–999 differentials have no disagreements; Lean's four known signature classes
retain the exact integration15 counts. Whole-protocol/device freshness,
general aggregate/time reductions, actual unauthenticated/revoked-device
campaigns and the repaired 015 custody workflow remain explicit gaps in
`../issues/20261018-sb4-security-proof-and-adversarial-qualification-gaps.md`.
This is a conservative verification foundation, not a zero-holes capability
claim. No version bump, push or publication.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG staging clock control

Classified staging stores admit serve --clock-offset and the existing clock
advance/status wire under a full service token. Production refuses offsets
before listener startup and all HTTP clock control even with operator authority.
The writer rechecks classification/session before advancing. startServe exposes
clockOffset, now and advance; it mints a current staging-clock operator session
for each control rather than caching a token across arbitrarily large jumps.
Fixture/reset/persona/job dev doors remain closed. The native-backed client
regression passed, including CLI advancement and restart dropping the offset.
The installed-package staging qualifier now exercises the same clock helpers.
LLP 3000.000.000.002 records the combined MIG language/adoption amendment;
independent review continues before final acceptance.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG declarative backfills and device bytes

Backfills are typed scalar patches evaluated before candidate narrowing, with
per-lifetime preimages and declaration membership retained through dormancy.
The existing activation transaction stages every result, checks historical
lookup uniqueness, then publishes data/indexes/derived state and generation.
Whole-table and narrowed restoration join the source snapshot. Hot adoption
and exact previews sample the serving clock, including staging offsets.

The web schema omits server-only migration metadata and evidence. Locked Linux
measurement is 2,385,285 B raw / 726,681 B gzip, +4,796 raw / +679 gzip versus
Integration 17. The small deterministic text library must use the same Rust
interpreter on server and device; the artifact allowance moves to measured
727,181 B (exactly 500 B headroom). No runtime meter ceiling changes. Family
and absent-feature audits and final qualification are recorded in the MIG
report; this measurement alone does not claim those checks have passed.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/dormant.rs:2:// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#2-retain-by-default — moves, restorations and deletion evidence share activation's transaction.

     1	//! Release backfills use the same activation transaction as retention and indexes.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#3-adoption-and-reversibility — stage every result before mutating the input snapshot.
     3	use super::*;
     4	use crate::{schema::{backfill::Backfill, materialize_optional_fields}, engine::{Context, runner::Runner}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}};
     5	use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
     6	pub(crate) const VERSION: &str = "snapback4-release-store-4";
     7	fn refusal(message: impl Into<String>) -> RuntimeError { RuntimeError::Structural { code:"E_ADOPT", message:message.into() } }
     8	fn present(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
     9	    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?)
    10	}
    11	fn enable(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    12	    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_evidence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,inputs TEXT NOT NULL,before_image TEXT NOT NULL,after_image TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    13	    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_pruned(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    14	    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_definitions(digest TEXT PRIMARY KEY,definition TEXT NOT NULL);")?;
    15	    tx.set_meta("version", VERSION)
    16	}
    17	#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
    18	struct Evidence { active:bool, inputs: Value, before: BTreeMap<String,Value>, after: BTreeMap<String,Value> }
    19	fn evidence(tx: &Tx<'_>, t:i64,id:&str,key:&str) -> Result<Option<Evidence>,RuntimeError> {
    20	    let value:Option<(String,String,String,bool)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT inputs,before_image,after_image,active FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?,r.get(3)?))).optional()?;
    21	    value.map(|(inputs,before,after,active)|Ok(Evidence {active,inputs:decode(&inputs)?,before:decode(&before)?,after:decode(&after)?})).transpose()
    22	}
    23	fn decode<T:serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(s:&str)->Result<T,RuntimeError>{serde_json::from_str(s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))}
    24	fn save(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str,e:&Evidence,active:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    25	    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_evidence VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7) ON CONFLICT(t,id,definition) DO UPDATE SET inputs=excluded.inputs,before_image=excluded.before_image,after_image=excluded.after_image,active=excluded.active",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&e.inputs),canonical_json(&e.before),canonical_json(&e.after),active])?;
    26	    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key])?;
    27	    Ok(())
    28	}
    29	pub(super) fn delete(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    30	    if present(tx)? {
    31	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    32	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    33	    } Ok(())
    34	}
    35	// Never archive reference/asset preimages without lifetime evidence. New reference
    36	// fields are safe: subsequent retirement is owned by the ordinary dormant ledger.
    37	fn protected(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    38	    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    39	    match kind {Optional(k)=>protected(k),Ref{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}=>true,_=>false}
    40	}
    41	pub(super) fn transition(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,now:i64,report:&mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    42	    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) && !present(tx)? { return Ok(()); }
    43	    enable(tx)?;
    44	    for fill in &next.backfills { fill.admit(next).map_err(refusal)?; }
    45	    // A name is immutable within a continuous lineage. Replacing a transform
    46	    // deliberately goes through an old-source/bridge transition first.
    47	    if let Some(old)=previous {for f in &next.backfills {if old.backfills.iter().any(|p|p.table==f.table && p.name==f.name && p.key()!=f.key()) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} changed; use a new name in a bridge release rather than replacing historical meaning",f.table,f.name)));}}}
    48	    for fill in &next.backfills {
    49	        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_definitions VALUES(?1,?2)",params![fill.key(),canonical_json(fill)])?;
    50	    }
    51	    let next_keys:BTreeSet<_>=next.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key).collect();
    52	    let definitions:Vec<Backfill>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition FROM _backfill_definitions WHERE digest IN (SELECT definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 UNION SELECT definition FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE active=1) ORDER BY digest")?.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))?.map(|s|decode(&s?)).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    53	    for fill in definitions.iter().rev().filter(|f|!next_keys.contains(&f.key())) {
    54	        let Some(table)=next.table(&fill.table) else {continue};
    55	        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
    56	        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
    57	            let Some(e)=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)? else {
    58	                let pruned:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3 AND active=1)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0))?;
    59	                if pruned && fill.outputs.iter().any(|c|table.columns.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,"rollback preimage was explicitly pruned; select a bridge preserving current data or restore a backup");}
    60	                return Ok(())
    61	            };
    62	            if !e.active {return Ok(())}
    63	            let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid stored row"))};
    64	            let mut changed=false;
    65	            for c in &fill.outputs {if table.columns.contains_key(c) {
    66	                let current=fields.get(c);let before=e.before.get(c);let after=e.after.get(c);
    67	                if current!=after && current!=before {bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} changed after backfill {}; keep a bridge and repair through an authorized mutation before rollback",fill.table,c,fill.name));return Ok(())}
    68	                if let Some(value)=before {fields.insert(c.clone(),value.clone());}else{fields.remove(c);}
    69	                changed=true;
    70	            }}
    71	            if changed {tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;count+=1;}
    72	            save(tx,t,id,&key,&e,false)
    73	        })?;
    74	        if bad.count>0 {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} rollback: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
    75	        if count>0 {report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} reversed on {count} rows",fill.table,fill.name));tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;}
    76	    }
    77	    let old_keys:BTreeSet<_>=previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s|s.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key)).collect();
    78	    let restored_tables:BTreeSet<i64>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT n.t FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=n.t AND r.id=n.id)")?.query_map([],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    79	    let entering:Vec<_>=next.backfills.iter().filter(|f|!old_keys.contains(&f.key()) || tx.num(&f.table).is_ok_and(|t|restored_tables.contains(&t))).collect();
    80	    if entering.is_empty(){return Ok(())}
    81	    let populated = entering.iter().try_fold(false, |any,fill| -> Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    82	        Ok(any || tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t=?1)",[tx.num(&fill.table)?],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?)
    83	    })?;
    84	    if !populated {return Ok(())}
    85	    let mut source=previous.cloned().unwrap_or_default();source.tables=sources.clone();source.backfills.clear();
    86	    // Point lookup indexes are rebuilt against the assembled source images,
    87	    // including restored rows, before any output patch is applied.
    88	    let mut reads=BTreeSet::new();
    89	    for f in &entering {collect_reads(&f.program.body,&mut reads);}
    90	    for (name,index) in reads {
    91	        let wanted=next.table(&name).and_then(|t|t.index(&index)).ok_or_else(||refusal("backfill lookup index absent"))?;
    92	        let old_table=source.tables.get_mut(&name).ok_or_else(||refusal(format!("backfill lookup source {name} has no historical definition")))?;
    93	        if !wanted.is_plain() || wanted.columns().iter().any(|c|c!="id" && !old_table.columns.contains_key(c)) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill lookup {name}.{index} requires predecessor fields; use an indexed bridge")))}
    94	        old_table.indexes.insert(index.clone(),wanted.clone());
    95	        let old=&source.tables[&name].indexes[&index];
    96	        let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;
    97	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
    98	        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(&name)?,|id,mut row|{
    99	            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[&name]);
   100	            for key in index_keys(old,&row,id) {tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;} Ok(())
   101	        })?;
   102	        if old.unique {validate_lookup(tx,ti,old.components.len(),&name,&index)?;}
   103	    }
   104	    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _backfill_pending(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,patch TEXT NOT NULL,evidence TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
   105	    for fill in entering {
   106	        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let table=&next.tables[&fill.table];let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
   107	        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
   108	            if old_keys.contains(&key) && !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",params![t,id],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {return Ok(())}
   109	            let prior=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)?;
   110	            if prior.is_none() && previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|t|fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|!t.columns.contains_key(c)))
   111	                && fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|row.field(c).is_ok() && crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],row.field(c).ok()).is_ok()) {return Ok(())}
   112	            if let Some(table)=sources.get(&fill.table){materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);}
   113	            let mut historical=row.clone();
   114	            if let Some(prior)=prior.as_ref().filter(|e|e.active) {
   115	                if let Value::Row(fields)|Value::Map(fields)=&mut historical {
   116	                    for c in fill.inputs.keys().filter(|c|fill.outputs.contains(*c)) {
   117	                        if let Some(value)=prior.before.get(c) {fields.insert(c.clone(),value.clone());} else {fields.remove(c);}
   118	                    }
   119	                }
   120	            }
   121	            let mut args=BTreeMap::new();
   122	            for (c,kind) in &fill.inputs {
   123	                let value=historical.field(c).cloned().ok();
   124	                if crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,kind,value.as_ref()).is_err() {
   125	                    // Native rows can survive a return through an older source;
   126	                    // they have no historical input, but retain a valid v2 image.
   127	                    if prior.is_none() && fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],row.field(c).ok()).is_ok()) {return Ok(())}
   128	                    bad.add(id,&format!("historical input {}.{c} is absent or does not fit {kind}; use a typed bridge to repair it",fill.table));return Ok(())
   129	                }
   130	                args.insert(c.clone(),value.unwrap_or(Value::Null));
   131	            }
   132	            let inputs=Value::Row(args.clone().into());
   133	            let mut context=Context::system().at(now);context.args=args;
   134	            let mut runner=Runner::new(tx,&source,context,Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort),0);
   135	            let patch=match runner.run(&fill.program) {Ok(Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f))=>f.into_iter().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(),Ok(_)=>{bad.add(id,"backfill did not return an object");return Ok(())},Err(e)=>{bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}};
   136	            if runner.read_cut().is_some() {bad.add(id,"historical lookup did not complete; reduce per-row lookup work before adoption");return Ok(())}
   137	            if patch.keys().cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()!=fill.outputs {return Err(refusal("backfill returned undeclared output fields"))}
   138	            let fields=row.as_object()?;let mut before=prior.as_ref().filter(|e|e.active).map_or_else(BTreeMap::new,|e|e.before.clone());let mut desired=patch.clone();
   139	            for (c,value) in &patch {
   140	                if let Err(e)=crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],Some(value)) {bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}
   141	                if let Some(kind)=sources.get(&fill.table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(c)) {if protected(kind) && prior.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|e.before.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,&format!("cannot overwrite historical reference or asset {}.{c}; derive into a new column",fill.table));return Ok(())}}
   142	                if !prior.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.active) && (previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c)) || (previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_none() && sources.get(&fill.table).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c)))) {
   143	                    if let Some(v)=historical.field(c).ok(){before.insert(c.clone(),v.clone());}
   144	                }
   145	                if let Some(prior)=&prior {
   146	                    let deps_same=prior.inputs==inputs && prior.after==patch;
   147	                    let current=fields.get(c);
   148	                    let original=prior.before.get(c);
   149	                    if deps_same && (prior.active || original.is_none()) && current.is_some() {
   150	                        // Added columns retain edits under v1's dormant storage.
   151	                        desired.insert(c.clone(),current.unwrap().clone());
   152	                    } else if !deps_same && current!=prior.after.get(c) && current!=original {
   153	                        bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} was edited and its historical inputs changed; keep a bridge and resolve the conflict",fill.table,c));return Ok(())
   154	                    }
   155	                }
   156	            }
   157	            let e=Evidence{active:true,inputs,before,after:patch};
   158	            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO temp._backfill_pending VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&desired),canonical_json(&e)])?;count+=1;
   159	            Ok(())
   160	        })?;
   161	        if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {}: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
   162	        if count>0{report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} transformed {count} rows",fill.table,fill.name));}
   163	    }
   164	    // Stream patches; avoid retaining the whole transformed population in RAM.
   165	    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition,patch,evidence FROM temp._backfill_pending ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
   166	    let mut rows=stmt.query([])?;
   167	    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
   168	        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;
   169	        let patch:BTreeMap<String,Value>=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(3)?)?;let e:Evidence=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(4)?)?;
   170	        let data:String=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0))?;let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;
   171	        if let Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=&mut row {f.extend(patch);}
   172	        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
   173	        save(tx,t,&id,&key,&e,true)?;
   174	    }
   175	    drop(rows);drop(stmt);
   176	    tx.conn().execute_batch("DROP TABLE temp._backfill_pending;")?;
   177	    // The source-index rebuild and every transformed dependency require full
   178	    // candidate key/cap/maintain validation, even when its declaration is equal.
   179	    tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;
   180	    Ok(())
   181	}
   182	fn collect_reads(body:&[crate::ir::Stmt],out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){
   183	    use crate::ir::{Expr,Stmt};
   184	    fn expr(e:&Expr,out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){match e{
   185	        Expr::Scan(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for k in &r.prefix{if let Some(e)=k.scalar(){expr(e,out);}}if let Some(e)=&r.filter{expr(e,out);}},
   186	        Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for e in &r.key{expr(e,out);}},
   187	        Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base,out),Expr::Array(es)|Expr::Builtin{args:es,..}=>{for e in es{expr(e,out);}},
   188	        Expr::Object(fs)=>{for e in fs.values(){expr(e,out);}},Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>{expr(base,out);for e in fields.values(){expr(e,out);}},
   189	        Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>{expr(left,out);expr(right,out);},Expr::Block{body,value}=>{collect_reads(body,out);expr(value,out);},_=>()
   190	    }}
   191	    for s in body{match s{Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value,out),Stmt::Require{condition,..}=>expr(condition,out),Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>{expr(condition,out);collect_reads(then_body,out);collect_reads(else_body,out);},_=>()}}
   192	}
   193	
   194	// Prune forgets historical values, retaining only the fact that exact reversal
   195	// is no longer available. No tombstone can be queried by an app.
   196	pub(super) fn prune(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   197	    if present(tx)? {tx.conn().execute_batch("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_pruned SELECT t,id,definition,active FROM _backfill_evidence; DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence;")?;}
   198	    Ok(())
   199	}
   200	pub(super) fn inventory_sql(conn:&Connection)->Result<&'static str,RuntimeError>{
   201	    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
   202	    Ok(if exists {" UNION ALL SELECT c.name||'.backfill#'||substr(e.definition,1,12),'backfill',c.name,e.definition FROM _backfill_evidence e JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=e.t GROUP BY e.t,e.definition "}else{""})
   203	}
   204	
   205	pub(super) fn has_evidence(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{present(tx)}
   206	pub(super) fn columns(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema)->Result<BTreeMap<String,BTreeSet<String>>,RuntimeError>{
   207	    let mut out:BTreeMap<String,BTreeSet<String>>=BTreeMap::new();
   208	    for f in &next.backfills {out.entry(f.table.clone()).or_default().extend(f.outputs.iter().cloned());}
   209	    if present(tx)? {
   210	        let mut statement=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition FROM _backfill_definitions WHERE digest IN (SELECT definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 UNION SELECT definition FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE active=1)")?;
   211	        for text in statement.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))? {
   212	            let f:Backfill=decode(&text?)?;out.entry(f.table).or_default().extend(f.outputs);
   213	        }
   214	    }
   215	    Ok(out)
   216	}
   217	fn validate_lookup(tx:&Tx<'_>,ti:i64,prefix:usize,table:&str,index:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   218	    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT k,id FROM i WHERE ti=?1 ORDER BY k")?;let mut rows=stmt.query([ti])?;
   219	    let mut prior=None;let mut first=String::new();let mut duplicate=false;let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();
   220	    while let Some(row)=rows.next()? {
   221	        let key:Vec<u8>=row.get(0)?;let id:String=row.get(1)?;let mut group=crate::key::decode(&key).ok_or_else(||refusal("invalid historical lookup key"))?;group.truncate(prefix);
   222	        if group.contains(&Value::Null){continue;}
   223	        if prior.as_ref()==Some(&group) {
   224	            if !duplicate {bad.add(&first,"duplicate historical lookup key");duplicate=true;}
   225	            bad.add(&id,"duplicate historical lookup key");
   226	        }else{prior=Some(group);first=id;duplicate=false;}
   227	    }
   228	    if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("historical lookup {table}.{index} is not unique: {}; prepare a unique bridge before deriving values",bad.describe())))}
   229	    Ok(())
   230	}
