/// The type an argument declares as a column type, for value validation.
pub fn arg_as_column(kind: &ArgType) -> Option<ColumnType> {
    Some(match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor | ArgType::Scalar => return None,
        ArgType::Id => ColumnType::Id,
        ArgType::Principal => ColumnType::Principal,
        ArgType::Ref { table } => ColumnType::Ref { table: table.clone() },
        ArgType::Text { max, .. } => ColumnType::Text { max: *max },
        ArgType::Bytes { max } => ColumnType::Bytes { max: *max },
        ArgType::Image { max } => ColumnType::Image { max: *max, once: false },
        ArgType::Video { max } => ColumnType::Video { max: *max },
        ArgType::File { max, types } => ColumnType::File { max: *max, types: types.clone() },
        ArgType::Int => ColumnType::Int,
        ArgType::IntRange { min, max } => ColumnType::IntRange { min: *min, max: *max },
        ArgType::Bool => ColumnType::Bool,
        ArgType::Time => ColumnType::Time,
        ArgType::Json { max_bytes } => ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: *max_bytes },
        ArgType::Optional(inner) => ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(arg_as_column(inner)?)),
//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — member reads share the row-read and field-disclosure capability.
    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.024#decision — this id came from an authorized head in
        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,
        // then recheck the rule with a separate witness reservation. Neither
        // hidden/expired images nor private fields can select actual-size admission.
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        if trusted && !self.device && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive() && table.recorded.is_empty()
            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };
            let materialization = row.materialization_heap();
            let prepay = self.meter.mark();
            self.meter.heap(materialization)?;
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let row = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner,_| {
                let row = runner.authorize_image(name,row.into_value())?;
                if let Some(row) = &row { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); }
                Ok(row)
            });
            // The value now owns the default. Transfer its prepay to charge(),
            // keeping only one retained reservation after the recheck.
            self.meter.release_row_heap(prepay, materialization);
            let row = row?;
            if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
            return row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose();
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let row = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, 1), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner, scope| {
            let Some(mut row) = runner.tx.get(scope, name, id)? else { return Ok(None); };
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let until = table.deadline(&row);
            let authorized = runner.authorize_image(name, row)?;
            if let Some(row) = &authorized { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); runner.next_expiry.note_option(until); }
            Ok(authorized)
        })?;
        if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
        row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose()
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied or expired candidate's witness deadlines are discarded with its scope.
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if self.inactive(name, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
        if !self.check_rule(name, Effect::Read, None, &row)? {
            self.hidden_row();
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Memory deadlines are hidden by Disclosed itself; transfer the live
        // boundary before projecting, still inside this candidate's expiry scope.
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.note_expiry(table, &row); }
        let projection = self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();
        let visible = projection.into_value();
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: expiry.defer() }))
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_disclosed(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized(name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,true,true,#[cfg(feature = "server-search")] None)
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_authorized(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized_prepaid(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered, trusted, false, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — a delivered uniform prefix has prepaid its
    // logical rule once; provisional rows still undergo physical authorization.
    pub(super) fn scan_authorized_prepaid(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // A public, non-expiring image is authorized before accessing storage.
        // No hidden candidate can influence its actual-size decoder admission.
        // Trusted selected heads may use the same actual-size adapter. It
        // decodes a full transient image; the streaming consumer retains only
        // its ordering key. Selection remains useful in the private decoder.
        if (self.context.is_system() || trusted && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive()) && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a| a.audience) {
            if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>,Vec<u8>,
                )>(limit))?; }
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            let live = table.read_lifetime_sensitive();
            // A web device never reads as the system, the only reader of an expiring table here.
            #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
            let live = false;
            let rows = if live { self.live_store_scan(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered)? }
            else if metered {
                self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,&mut runner.meter)
                })?
            } else { self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit)
                })? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.5 — a device prices the server's image with the server's charge. Its
            // own prediction rows still meet a non-uniform rule, as prepaid internal work outside the caller's
            // heap; after an earlier cut such a row stays hidden (the read is incomplete either way).
            let check = self.device && !self.context.is_system() && !table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable();
            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.len());
            for (row, key) in rows {
                let row = if check && self.is_provisional(name, &row)? {
                    self.prepaid_scan(&[], false, |runner, scope| {
                        runner.candidate_verdict(scope, name, index, prefix, row, true, rule_prepaid)
                    })?
                } else { Some(AuthorizedRow { projection:self.disclosed_row(table,row),authority:RowAuthorization(()),expiry:Default::default() }) };
                out.push((row, key));
            }
            return Ok(out);
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>, Vec<u8>,
            )>(limit))?; }
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(limit);
        for _ in 0..limit {
            let candidate = self.reserved_scratch(bytes, &[], !trusted, |runner, scope| {
                loop {
                #[cfg(not(feature = "server-search"))]
                let selected = runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,1)?;
                #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
                let selected = if let Some(fields) = fields { runner.tx.scan_selected(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,fields)? } else { runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,1)? };
                let Some((row,key)) = selected.into_iter().next() else { return Ok(None); };
                // Advancing over a rejected candidate is internal decoder work.
                cursor.after = crate::key::decode(&key).map(|v|v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect());
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — an expired, unswept candidate is never returned, counted or charged.
                if runner.step_inactive(name, &row, &key)? { continue; }
                return Ok(Some((runner.candidate_verdict(scope, name, index, prefix, row, trusted, rule_prepaid)?,key,
                    )));
                }
            })?;
            let Some((row,key)) = candidate else { break; };
            // Only this sealed surviving projection can be priced on the caller ledger.
            if metered && let Some(row) = &row {
                self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes() + row.projection_heap() + row.materialization_heap()
                    + crate::rows::word_heap::key_decode(table.index(index).map_or(1,|i|i.components.len()+1),key.len()))?;
            }
            out.push((row,key));
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// A system read of an expiring table takes the store path, so the store
    /// would price every fetched entry. Open the authored range once, step
    /// over expired entries internally, and price live entries through the
    /// same canonical-image codec used by every store.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — the public meter charges only rows live at the read's clock.
    #[cfg_attr(not(feature = "sqlite"), allow(dead_code))]
    fn live_store_scan(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        // Price the authored range once. Internal cursors over expired keys
        // cannot select another public scan-opening charge or wider bounds.
        let mut guard = super::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut self.meter, |meter, scope| {
            if metered { crate::rows::word_heap::scan_open(meter, name, index, prefix, bounds, scope)?; }
            Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(crate::rows::word_heap::StoreGuard::open(meter, super::read::EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD))
        })?;
//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

    pub(crate) fn dev_doors(&self) -> bool {

    }

        }
    }

//! Referential actions: what deleting a referenced row does to the rows that
//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.
use super::*;
pub use reservations::Price;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum OnDelete {
    Restrict,
    Cascade,
    SetNull,
    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    /// their target.
    Keep,
}

impl OnDelete {
    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    }
    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
}

/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    pub child: &'a str,
    pub table: &'a Table,
    pub column: &'a str,
    pub action: OnDelete,
}

/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }

/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;

impl Schema {
    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }

    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }

    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
        }
        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
            let columns = index.columns();
            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    }

    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        let declared = table.constraints.iter().find_map(|c| match c {
            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.len() == 1 && by[0] == column => Some(*max),
            _ => None,
        });
        let unique = table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns() == [column]).then_some(1);
        declared.into_iter().chain(unique).min()
    }

    /// Whether `cap` on `table` skips a group whose reference is null: only a
    /// `set null` reference's own cap does (a null reference has no parent).
    pub fn cap_skips_null(&self, table: &Table, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> bool {
        by.iter().zip(group).any(|(column, value)| *value == Value::Null && table.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    /// The tables a delete of a `parent` row may write through its writing
//! Row authority and dormant cell provenance are separate facts.
// @ref LLP 3000.014.000#implementation-proof-clarifications — validate the immediate manual policy before using older live evidence.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::through::{PARENT_DEADLINE, DEADLINE};

pub(super) fn mode(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<Option<u8>,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(None);}
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT CASE kind WHEN 'source-manual' THEN 0 WHEN 'source-captured' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind IN ('source-manual','source-captured','source-live') AND t=?1 AND col='' AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?)
}
pub(super) fn record(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(());}
    let kind=match &old.through {None=>"source-manual",Some(e) if e.live.is_some()=>"source-live",Some(_)=>"source-captured"};
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES(?1,?2,'',?3)",params![kind,t,id])?;
    if let Some(edge)=old.through.as_ref().filter(|e|e.live.is_none()) {
        for col in edge.fields.keys().map(String::as_str).chain([PARENT_DEADLINE,DEADLINE]) {
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('source-capture',?1,?2,?3)",params![t,col,id])?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn live_cell(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,col:&str)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND live_source=1)",params![t,id,col],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
/// Captured-only restoration preserves its old audience. A manual row with
/// live-origin inputs must prove its *current* audience before recapture.
pub(super) fn validate(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,name:&str,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64,narrowed:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let Some(table)=next.table(name) else {return Ok(())};
    let t=tx.num(name)?;let id=row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
    if needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,narrowed)? {
        crate::engine::through_activation::forward_authority(tx,next,sources,name,old,row,now,true)?;
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('live-authority',?1,'',?2)",params![t,id])?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
/// Never let unused live cells replace a captured row's own audience/deadline.
/// Missing live-origin inputs remain absent until the capture barrier fills
/// just those new inputs from the current parent.
pub(super) fn overlay(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table,row:&mut Value,values:Vec<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let captured=old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).filter(|e|e.live.is_none());
    for (col,data) in values {
        if !table.columns.contains_key(&col) {continue;}
        let protected=captured.is_some_and(|e|e.fields.contains_key(&col) || matches!(col.as_str(),PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE));
        if protected {continue;}
        let stale=captured.is_some() && live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,&col)?;
        if let Value::Row(fields)|Value::Map(fields)=row {
            if stale {fields.remove(&col);} else {fields.insert(col,serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn live_input(table:&Table,column:&str)->bool {
    table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || matches!(column,PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE)))
}
/// Current policy inputs are refreshable; the immutable parent lifetime and
/// authored references remain bound even when old policy targets disappear.
pub(super) fn obsolete_ref(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,column:&str,table:&Table,old:Option<&Table>)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    let Some(edge)=&table.through else {return Ok(false)};
    if column==PARENT_DEADLINE || !(edge.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE) {return Ok(false);}
    let protected=if mode(tx,t,id)?.is_some() {
        tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind='source-capture' AND t=?1 AND id=?2 AND col=?3)",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?
    } else {old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_none() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE))};
    if protected {return Ok(false);}
    Ok(edge.live.is_some() || live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,column)?)
}

/// Reserve declared parent scratch only for callbacks that can fetch it.
/// Captured-only restoration keeps its existing actual-image admission.
pub(super) fn forward_fetch(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    match old {Some(old)=>needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,false),None=>Ok(false)}
}
                    .map(|i| serde_json::to_value(i).unwrap())
                    .collect();
            }
        }
        let workers:Vec<(String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key='worker:status' OR key LIKE 'worker:status:%' ORDER BY key LIMIT 16")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        let previous_active: Option<u64> = if present(tx.conn())? {tx.conn().query_row("SELECT previous FROM _release_events WHERE release IS NOT NULL AND previous != release AND operation != 'prune' ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1",[],|r|r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(0)).optional()?.flatten().map(|n|n as u64)} else {None};
        let result = serde_json::json!({"previous_active":previous_active,"release":tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()),"revision":tx.meta("release_revision")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"generation":tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(1),"native_digest":tx.meta("release_native_digest")?,"events_after":events.last().map(|e|e["id"].clone()),"events":events,"items_after":inventory_page.last().map(|i|i["key"].clone()),"items":inventory_page,"event":event_id,"workers":workers});

    }
    pub fn prune_dormant(
        &mut self,
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.transaction()?;
        let held = tx
            .meta("release_revision")?
            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
            .unwrap_or(0);
        if expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != held) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation(
                "release revision changed; run releases and retry prune".into(),
            ));
        }
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), None, usize::MAX)?;
        if !confirm {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(
                serde_json::json!({"pruned":false,"items":items,"rewrite":"snapback4 prune <dir> --allow-destructive"}),
            );
        }
        if !present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":[],"revision":held}));
        }
        // Release only dormant homes; active application data is untouched.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE home_table IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=v.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column AND v.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
        if super::narrowed::present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",[])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows",[])?;
        }
        let revision = event(
            &tx,
            "prune",
            tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v| v.parse().ok()),
            None,
            serde_json::json!({"dropped_items":items.len(),"bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(),"asset_bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.asset_bytes).sum::<u64>()}),
            &items,
        )?;
        let event_id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
        super::backfills::prune(&tx)?;
        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _dormant_values;DELETE FROM _dormant_rows;DELETE FROM _dormant_refs;DELETE FROM _dormant_columns;DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes;DELETE FROM _dormant_tables;")?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":items,"revision":revision,"event":event_id}))
    }
}
            bounded(value)
        ));

+pub(super) fn retire_sources(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>,now:i64)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
+    // An expired predecessor must not be archived and later revived without its lifetime.
+    if let Some(previous) = previous {
+        let retirement = crate::engine::through_activation::Retirement::new(next, previous, now);
+        for (name, old) in &previous.tables {
+            if next.tables.contains_key(name) && retirement.affects(name) {
+                super::activation::walk(tx, tx.num(name)?, |_, row| {
+                    if retirement.retires(name, &row) {
+                        tx.delete(name, old, &row)?;
+                    }
+                    Ok(())
+                })?;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    Ok(())
+}

+// Restore retained source images before backfill evaluation. Candidate-owned
+// output cells are checked after transformation, so a same-column enum rename
+// does not preclude its own source image from entering the snapshot.
+pub(super) fn restore_images(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,now:i64,source_definitions:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,authority_sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,for_backfills:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
+    let rewritten=if for_backfills {super::backfills::columns(tx,next)?}else{Default::default()};
+    let mut cursor = (0i64, String::new());
+    loop {
+        let item: Option<(i64,String,i64,i64)> = tx.conn().query_row(
+            "SELECT t,id,octet_length(data),octet_length(definition) FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE (t,id)>(?1,?2) AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_dormant_narrow_rows.t AND r.id=_dormant_narrow_rows.id) ORDER BY t,id LIMIT 1",
+            params![cursor.0,cursor.1],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?,r.get(3)?))).optional()?;
+        let Some((t,id,data_bytes,definition_bytes))=item else {break};
+        // Retain one admitted provenance/image unit through decode, overlays,
+        // reconciliation and encoding. Never own its blobs before this bound.
+        let mut admission=crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::job(),crate::meter::Cut::Abort);
+        if super::live_activation::active(tx)? {
+            admission.heap((data_bytes as u64).saturating_add(definition_bytes as u64).saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;
+        }
+        let (data,definition):(String,String)=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data,definition FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?;
+        cursor = (t, id.clone());
+        let name: String =
+            tx.conn()
+                .query_row("SELECT name FROM _catalog WHERE num=?1", [t], |r| r.get(0))?;
+        let Some(table) = next
+            .table(&name)
+            .filter(|t| t.ephemeral.is_none() && next.maintain_for(&name).is_none())
+        else {
+            continue;
+        };
+        let old: Table =
+            serde_json::from_str(&definition).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
+        super::dormant::check_owner(tx, &name, &old, next)?;
+        let mut row: Value =
+            serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
+        crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&old);
+        let mut authority=crate::engine::through_activation::manual_authority_fields(next,&name,&old);
+        if for_backfills { authority.extend(rewritten.get(&name).into_iter().flatten().cloned()); }
+        if incompatible_except(tx,next,&name,&row,&authority)? {
+            continue;
+        }
+        // Retained source deadlines precede projection, including removal of read-through itself.
+        if crate::engine::through_activation::Retirement::retires_image(table,&old,&row,now) {
+            tx.delete(&name,&old,&row)?;
+            continue;
+        }
+        // Actual deletion/reuse never recaptures a different parent lifetime.
+        let invalid_columns = tx
+            .conn()
+            .prepare_cached(
+                "SELECT column_name FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND invalid=1",
+            )?
+            .query_map(params![t, id], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))?
+            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
+        let mut invalid=false;
+        for c in &invalid_columns {
+            if table.columns.contains_key(c) && !super::authority_origin::obsolete_ref(tx,t,&id,c,table,Some(&old))? {invalid=true;}
+        }
+        if invalid {
+            continue;
+        }
+        if super::authority_origin::live_row(tx,t,&id,&old,table)? {
+            admission.heap(crate::engine::through_activation::restored_parent_scratch(next,source_definitions,&name,&old).saturating_add(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_mul(4)))?;
+        }
+        super::authority_origin::record(tx,t,&id,&old)?;
+        super::authority_origin::validate(tx,next,authority_sources,&name,&old,&row,now,true)?;
+        crate::engine::through_activation::forward_image(tx,next,authority_sources,&name,&old,&row,now)?;
+        if old.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some()) {tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored(kind,t,col,id) VALUES('live-authority',?1,'',?2)",params![t,id])?;}
+        for column in old.columns.keys() {
+            if !table.columns.contains_key(column) {
+                super::dormant::definition(tx, &name, &old, column)?;
+                if let Ok(value) = row.field(column) {
+                    super::dormant::save_cell(tx, &name, &old, &id, column, value, false)?;
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if !for_backfills { if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
+            fields.retain(|c, _| table.columns.contains_key(c));
+        }}
+        let values=super::dormant::restored_values(tx,t,&id,table,&mut admission)?;
+        super::authority_origin::overlay(tx,t,&id,Some(&old),table,&mut row,values)?;
+        if old.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|e.live.is_some()) {
+            for column in table.columns.keys() {super::dormant::mark_live_column(tx,t,&id,column)?;}
+        }
+        crate::engine::through_activation::manual_image(tx,next,&name,&old,&mut row,now)?;
+        if if for_backfills {incompatible_except(tx,next,&name,&row,&authority)?}else{incompatible(tx,next,&name,&row)?} {continue;}
+        if !for_backfills {crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);}
+        // Missing required new fields are a genuine backfill refusal, not dormancy.
+        tx.conn().execute(
+            "INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",
+            params![t, id, canonical_json(&row)],
+        )?;
+    }
+    tx.clear_activation_scan();
+    Ok(())

   186	
   187	    fn changes_lifetime(next:&Table,old:&Table) -> bool {
   188	        next.through != old.through || next.through.is_some() && (next.expire != old.expire || next.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field)))
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    pub(crate) fn retires_image(next:&Table,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64) -> bool {
   192	        (next.through!=old.through || next.expire!=old.expire || old.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field))) && old.expired(row,now)
   193	    }
   194	
   195	    pub(crate) fn affects(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
   196	        self.affected.iter().any(|(table, _)| table == name)
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    pub(crate) fn retires(&self, name: &str, row: &Value) -> bool {
   200	        self.affected
   201	            .iter()
   202	            .find(|(table, _)| table == name)
   203	            .is_some_and(|(_, old)| old.expired(row, self.now))
   204	    }
   205	}

   207	/// Capture barrier for an authoritative adapter's uncommitted generation.
   208	pub fn activate(
   209	    tx: &dyn Rows,
   210	    next: &Schema,
   211	    previous: Option<&Schema>,
   212	    now: i64,
   213	) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, u64>, RuntimeError> {
   214	    activate_retained(tx, next, previous, now, &Default::default())
   215	}
   216	
   217	pub(crate) fn activate_retained(
   218	    tx: &dyn Rows,
   219	    next: &Schema,
   220	    previous: Option<&Schema>,
   221	    now: i64,
   222	    retained: &std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
   223	) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, u64>, RuntimeError> {
   224	    let mut shortened = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
   225	    let Some(previous) = previous else {
   226	        return Ok(shortened);
   227	    };
   228	    // Previous indexes and row images still exist. Every affected expired row
   229	    // retires under its old deadline, before a new default or capture can revive it.
   230	    let retirement = Retirement::new(next, previous, now);
   231	    for (name, old) in retirement.affected.iter().rev() {
   232	        let bounded=live_work(tx,name,Some(old),next)?;
   233	        rows(tx, name, old, 0, bounded, None, |row| {
   234	            if retirement.retires(name, &row) {
   235	                tx.delete(name, old, &row)?;
   236	            }
   237	            Ok(())
   238	        })?;
   239	    }
   240	    forward_sources(tx,next,&previous.tables,now)?;
   241	    reconcile_manual(tx,next,&previous.tables,now)?;
   242	    // Compare all predecessor images before any ancestor is recaptured.
   243	    for (name, _) in &retirement.affected {
   244	        let Some(t) = &next.tables[name].through else {
   245	            continue;
   313	            return Ok(
   314	                serde_json::json!({"pruned":false,"items":items,"rewrite":"snapback4 prune <dir> --allow-destructive"}),
   315	            );
   316	        }
   317	        if !present(tx.conn())? {
   318	            tx.rollback()?;
   319	            return Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":[],"revision":held}));
   320	        }
   321	        // Release only dormant homes; active application data is untouched.
   322	        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE home_table IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=v.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column AND v.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
   323	        if super::narrowed::present(tx.conn())? {
   324	            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
   325	            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",[])?;
   326	            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows",[])?;
   327	        }
   328	        let revision = event(
   329	            &tx,
   330	            "prune",
   331	            tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v| v.parse().ok()),
   332	            None,
   333	            serde_json::json!({"dropped_items":items.len(),"bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(),"asset_bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.asset_bytes).sum::<u64>()}),
   334	            &items,
   335	        )?;
   336	        let event_id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
   337	        super::backfills::prune(&tx)?;
   338	        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _dormant_values;DELETE FROM _dormant_rows;DELETE FROM _dormant_refs;DELETE FROM _dormant_columns;DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes;DELETE FROM _dormant_tables;")?;
   339	        tx.commit()?;
   340	        Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":items,"revision":revision,"event":event_id}))
   341	    }
   342	}
            t.through
                .as_ref()
                .filter(|c| c.parent == parent)
                .map(|c| (name, t, c))
        })
    }
    /// Stable ancestor order; admission rejects cycles before execution.
    pub fn through_order(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        fn depth(schema: &Schema, name: &str, seen: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> usize {
            if !seen.insert(name.into()) {
                return 0;
            }
            schema
                .table(name)
                .and_then(|t| t.through.as_ref())
                .map_or(0, |t| 1 + depth(schema, &t.parent, seen))
        }
        let mut names: Vec<_> = self.tables.keys().cloned().collect();
        names.sort_by_key(|name| (depth(self, name, &mut BTreeSet::new()), name.clone()));
        names
    }
}

    let mut issues = Vec::new();

        let Some(t) = &table.through else {
            continue;
        };
        let mut refuse = |message: String| {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue {
                path: format!("tables.{name}.through"),
                message,
            })

//! Typed admission of rows and arguments at the store's trust boundary. A
//! value that does not fit its declared type is refused before any rule or
//! constraint sees it.

use crate::error::RuntimeError;
use crate::ir::{ArgType, Program};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Constraint, Schema, TextFormat, contains_system_principal};
use crate::value::{I53_MAX, I53_MIN, Value, canonical_json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub fn validate_row(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, false)
}

/// Adoption builders still own recorded/captured slots. Check authoritative
/// fields before their work, and validate the complete image afterward.
#[cfg(feature="sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_adoption_input(schema:&Schema,name:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let table=&schema.tables[name];
    for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
        if !table.engine_field(column) {validate_column(name,column,kind,row.field(column).ok())?;}
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text{field,min,format}=constraint && !table.engine_field(field) && let Ok(Value::String(text))=row.field(field){validate_text(&format!("{name}.{field}"),text,*min,*format)?;}
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — row-view storage is a private numerical cache, not a disclosure.
// No certificate yet means no computed fields; public/input validation stays strict.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_maintained_cache(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let cache = schema.maintain_for(table_name).is_some_and(|m| m.row_key.is_some());
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, cache)
}

/// A predicting patch preserves unknown, untouched fields as omissions.
/// The authoritative writer always uses the complete-row validator.
pub(super) fn validate_projection(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, viewer, false, false)
}

/// Application input is checked before engine slots are prepared. The final
/// stored image must also pass validate_row, with every required slot present.
pub(super) fn validate_before_slots(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, true, false)
}

fn validate_image(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>, before_slots: bool, cache: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let table = schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
    let object = row.as_object()?;
    if !object.contains_key("id") {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name} row lacks id")));
    }
    if let Some(name) = object.keys().find(|name| !table.columns.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} row contains undeclared field {name}")));
    }
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if before_slots && crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(name) { continue; }
        if cache && matches!(name.as_str(), "value" | "recountPending") && !object.contains_key(name) { continue; }
        let value = object.get(name).or_else(|| table.maintained.contains_key(name).then_some(&Value::Int(0)));
        if value.is_none() && viewer.is_some_and(|viewer| table.written_by.get(name).is_some_and(|authority|
            !authority.audience && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(viewer.into())))) { continue; }
        validate_column(table_name, name, kind, value)?;
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text { field, min, format } = constraint
            && let Some(Value::String(text)) = object.get(field)
        {
            validate_text(&format!("{table_name}.{field}"), text, *min, *format).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Constraint(error.to_string().trim_start_matches("E_INPUT: ").to_owned()))?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_column(table_name: &str, name: &str, kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let label = format!("{table_name}.{name}");
    if let Some(value) = value {
        crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
        if contains_system_principal(value) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
        }
    }
    if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_control(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
    }
    validate_column_minimum(kind, value, &label)?;
    if !fits(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match value {
            None | Some(Value::Null) => format!("{label} is required ({kind})"),
            Some(value) => format!("{label} must be {kind}, not {}", describe(value)),
        }));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — nested/optional columns use the same minimum on server and device writes.
fn validate_column_minimum(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_column_minimum(inner, Some(value), label),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(items))) => {
            if let ColumnType::ListRange { min, .. } = kind { validate_list_minimum(label, items.len(), *min)?; }
            for item in items { validate_column_minimum(of, Some(item), label)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}

fn validate_list_minimum(label: &str, length: usize, min: u32) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if length < min as usize {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} {}; got {length}", if min == 1 { "item" } else { "items" })));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn describe(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(text) if text.chars().count() > 24 => format!("{} characters of text", text.chars().count()),
        Value::String(text) => format!("the text '{text}'"),
        Value::Id(id) => format!("the id '{id}'"),
        Value::Int(n) => format!("the number {n}"),
        Value::Bool(b) => format!("the boolean {b}"),
        Value::Null => "null".into(),
        Value::Bytes(b) => format!("{} bytes", b.len()),
        Value::Array(items) => format!("a list of {}", items.len()),
        Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => "an object".into(),
    }
}

pub fn validate_text(label: &str, text: &str, min: u32, format: Option<TextFormat>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#6-bakeoff-repairs — minimums measure evidence, while maxima still measure the complete value.
    let length = text.chars().filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace()).count();
    if length < min as usize {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} non-whitespace characters; got {length}")));
    }
    if let Some(format) = format
        && !format.matches(text)
    {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match format {
            TextFormat::Handle => format!("{label} must be a handle: lowercase letters, digits and underscores"),
            TextFormat::Url => format!("{label} must be an http or https URL"),
            TextFormat::Email => format!("{label} must be an ASCII email address with one @, a dot-safe local part, and valid domain labels"),
        }));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn disallowed_control(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Option<u32> {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => disallowed_control(inner, Some(value)),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.iter().find_map(|value| disallowed_control(of, Some(value))),
        (ColumnType::Text { .. }, Some(Value::String(text))) => disallowed_text_control(text),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Text forbids C0 controls except tab, LF and CR.
pub fn disallowed_text_control(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    text.chars().find(|c| c.is_control() && !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r')).map(|c| c as u32)
}

        (ColumnType::Optional(_), None | Some(Value::Null)) => true,

/// Bind one table. A refused line becomes a diagnostic and a `deny` (or an
/// absent sync), so one mistake does not cascade into every reader.
fn bind_table(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Table {
    let decl = &info.decl;
    let mut columns = BTreeMap::new();
    columns.insert("id".to_owned(), core::ColumnType::Id);
    for column in &decl.columns {
        if let TypeKind::Ref { table } = &column.type_.kind
            && !catalog.tables.contains_key(table)
        {
            diagnostics.push(type_error(&column.site, format!("column {}.{} references unknown table {table}", decl.name, column.name), name_rewrite(table, catalog.tables.keys().map(String::as_str), Some("declare the table, or use id for an opaque identity"))));
        }
        columns.insert(column.name.clone(), scalar_to_column(&column.type_));
    }
    let mut recorded = BTreeMap::new();
    for (path, kind) in &info.recorded {
        let (reference, column) = path.split_once('.').expect("admitted path");
        let TypeKind::Ref { table } = &info.columns[reference].kind else { unreachable!("admitted reference") };
        recorded.insert(path.clone(), core::RecordedField { reference: reference.into(), table: table.clone(), column: column.into() });
        columns.insert(path.clone(), scalar_to_column(kind));
    }

                    Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{} has no unique index over {} column{} matching this key; its indexes are {}", read.table, keys.len(), if keys.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, declared.join(", ")), format!("unique by{}: {}", keys.iter().map(|(c, _, _)| c.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "Column".into())).map(|c| title(&c)).collect::<String>(), keys.iter().map(|(c, _, _)| c.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "<column>".into())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) + if named && mode != ReadMode::Point { "  -- an index is needed when the named equalities do not cover a total unique key" } else { "" }))
                } else {

                    .repair_read(&read.site, changes))
            }
        }
    }

    fn key_ty(&self, info: &TableInfo, index: &ast::IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
        if index.computed.contains_key(column) { return Ty::Int; }
        if column == "id" {
            return Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone());
        }
        info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.3 — a seek constrains the first index column after the pinned prefix.
    pub(super) fn seek(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Option<ir::Range>> {
        if read.seeks.is_empty() { return Ok(None); }
        let mut range = ir::Range { gt: None, gte: None, lt: None, lte: None };
        for bound in &read.seeks {
            if index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) != Some(&bound.column) || read.matching.is_some() {
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek compares the first unpinned column of a plain index", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
            }
            let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column);
            let (value, ty) = self.field_value(&ObjectField { spread: false, name: bound.column.clone(), value: Some(bound.value.clone()), site: bound.site.clone() }, &expected, out)?;
            if !ty.comparable(&expected) || ty.is_optional() {
                let endpoint = print::expression(&bound.value);
                let rewrite = if ty.is_optional() {
                    format!("put the read inside `if {endpoint} != null:`, then `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                } else {
                    format!("use a present {} endpoint in `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", expected.required().name(), read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                };
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, format!("seek on {}.{} expects present {}, got {}", read.table, bound.column, expected.required().name(), ty.name()), rewrite));
            }
            let slot = match bound.op.as_str() { ">" => &mut range.gt, ">=" => &mut range.gte, "<" => &mut range.lt, "<=" => &mut range.lte, _ => unreachable!("parser seek comparison") };
            if slot.is_some() { return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek bound is repeated", "write one lower and one upper bound")); }
            *slot = Some(Box::new(self.snapshot(value, out)));
        }
        if (range.gt.is_some() && range.gte.is_some()) || (range.lt.is_some() && range.lte.is_some()) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek has at most one lower and one upper bound", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
        }
        // Null sorts below every present value. An upper-only nullable seek
        // must skip the entire null segment, including its id suffixes.
        if range.gt.is_none() && range.gte.is_none()
            && read.seeks.first().is_some_and(|bound| self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column).is_optional()) {

    pub(super) fn record_selection(&mut self, site: &str, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, result: &str, reason: &str) {
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let bindings = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(position, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(position) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let origin = if self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())) { "synthesized; " } else { "" };
        let source = if origin.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; read: {}", self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(read))) };
        let mut selection = format!("selected {} ({bindings}); result: {result}; {origin}{reason}{source}", index.name);
        if !self.variants.is_empty() {
            let when = self.variants.iter().map(|(name, present)| format!("{} {}", name.trim_start_matches("$intentPresent:").trim_start_matches("$intentSearch:"), if *present { "present" } else { "absent" })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
            selection = format!("when {when}: {selection}");
        }
        self.selections.entry(site.into()).and_modify(|prior| { if !prior.contains(&selection) { prior.push_str("; "); prior.push_str(&selection); } }).or_insert(selection);
    }

//! One order-preserving byte encoding for index keys, used by the SQLite
//! store on the server and (in week 3) by the IndexedDB store on a device,
//! so both hosts scan the same bytes in the same order: the value model's
//! total order (`Value::total_cmp`), with one tag byte per component and a
//! self-terminating body, so an encoded prefix is a byte prefix of every key
//! that extends it.

use crate::value::Value;
use std::cmp::Ordering;

const T_NULL: u8 = 0x01;
const T_FALSE: u8 = 0x02;
const T_TRUE: u8 = 0x03;
const T_INT: u8 = 0x04;
const T_STRING: u8 = 0x05;
const T_ID: u8 = 0x06;
const T_BYTES: u8 = 0x07;
/// Greater than any tag: the exclusive upper bound of a prefix range.
const T_END: u8 = 0xFF;

/// Encode a tuple of scalar components. Rows, arrays and maps are not index
/// components (the schema refuses them), so they encode as their canonical
/// JSON text to keep the function total.
pub fn encode(values: &[Value]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(values.len() * 12);
    for value in values {
        encode_one(value, &mut out);
    }
    out
}

fn encode_one(value: &Value, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
    match value {
        Value::Null => out.push(T_NULL),
        Value::Bool(false) => out.push(T_FALSE),
        Value::Bool(true) => out.push(T_TRUE),
        Value::Int(int) => {
            out.push(T_INT);
            // Flip the sign bit so two's complement sorts as unsigned bytes.
            out.extend_from_slice(&((*int as u64) ^ (1 << 63)).to_be_bytes());
        }
        Value::String(text) => {
            out.push(T_STRING);
            escape(text.as_bytes(), out);
        }
        Value::Id(id) => {
            out.push(T_ID);
            escape(id.as_bytes(), out);
        }
        Value::Bytes(bytes) => {
            out.push(T_BYTES);
            escape(bytes, out);
        }
        other => {
            out.push(T_STRING);
            escape(crate::value::canonical_json(other).as_bytes(), out);
        }
    }
}

    /// Strict compound children run in source order, before setup emitted by

    pub(super) fn snapshot(&mut self, value: Expr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Expr {
        if matches!(value, Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Input(Input::Viewer | Input::Now)) || lexical_value(&value) {
            return value;
        }
        let name = self.temp("value");
        out.push(Stmt::Let { name: name.clone(), value });
        ir::var(&name)
    }

/// Every stored key of a row in an index: one for a plain index; for an
/// index with a `words(..)` component, one per distinct token of the named
/// text columns, the token in the component's position (none when the
/// text has no token: the row is absent from that index, as a null key
/// would be from a point read). Every store writes and deletes exactly
/// these, on every host.
pub fn index_keys(index: &Index, row: &Value, id: &str) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
    if let Some(crate::schema::IndexComponent::Contribution(maintain)) = index.components.first() {
        return if maintain.selected(row) { vec![index_key(index, row, id)] } else { Vec::new() };
    }
    let Some((position, fields)) = index.word_component() else { return vec![index_key(index, row, id)];
    };
    let texts: Vec<&str> = fields.iter().filter_map(|field| row.field(field).ok().and_then(|value| value.as_str().ok())).collect();
    let tokens = crate::words::row_tokens(texts);
    let values = index_values(index, row);
    tokens
        .into_iter()
        .map(|token| {
            let mut components: Vec<Value> = values[..position].to_vec();
            components.push(Value::String(token));
            components.extend(values[position..].iter().cloned());
            components.push(Value::Id(id.to_owned()));
            key::encode(&components)
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Raw stored/confirmed images may predate required literal defaults.
pub fn stored_index_keys(table: &Table, index: &Index, row: &Value, id: &str) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
    index_keys(index, &crate::schema::row_image(row, table), id)
}

pub fn compile(source: &str) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, Json> {
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    if bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        Ok(bound)
    } else {
        Err(serde_json::to_value(&bound.diagnostics).unwrap())
    }
}

**NOT READY**

1. **P1 — Backfills can overwrite row identity.** [Output admission](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/backfill.rs:53) permits `id`. The compiler accepts `{id: replacement}`, but [patch application](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:170) updates only the JSON image, retaining the original SQLite key and evidence identity. This can corrupt point lookup, indexing and deletion behavior. Reject `id` at the shared schema admission boundary and add a regression.

2. **P1 — Dropped historical references bypass lifetime invalidation.** [Narrowed restoration](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/narrowed.rs:303) checks an invalid reference only if its column remains in the candidate. Backfills retain access to dropped historical fields. A dormant child whose parent was deleted and recreated can therefore copy its dropped reference into a new reference column—or look up the replacement parent—and activate successfully. Validate lifetime evidence for consumed historical inputs before exposing them to backfills, regardless of candidate column visibility.

3. **P1 — Source expiry is not reliably enforced before transformation.** [Pre-backfill retirement](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/narrowed.rs:232) depends on `Retirement::affects`, whose [predicate](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/through_activation.rs:187) excludes ordinary expiry changes when neither table uses read-through. An expired, unswept row can reach the transform; dropping expiry also makes its former deadline writable because admission checks only candidate lifetime fields. Rewriting that deadline can preserve an already-ended lifetime. Retire source-expired rows from their original images before evaluating patches, including restored sources.

4. **P1 — Pruned evidence permits double application and fabricated reversal.** [Re-entry](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:109) treats missing evidence as a fresh row without consulting `_backfill_pruned`. Sequence: transform `n:3 → 6`, prune while active, remove the table, then restore the same declaration. It computes `6 → 12`, saves `6` as the preimage and clears the pruned marker; subsequent reversal returns `6`, falsely presenting it as the original value. Re-entry must honor pruned membership and refuse when required historical values are unavailable.

5. **P2 — Pruning deleted dormant lifetimes leaves evidence attached to reusable IDs.** [Prune](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/release_history.rs:323) deletes narrowed rows, then [converts all evidence into pruned markers](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:196). Reusing a pruned row’s ID can inherit its active reversal-unavailable marker and incorrectly block rollback. Clear evidence and markers for lifetimes actually deleted by prune; preserve markers only for surviving lifetimes.

No separate material staging-clock-authority finding emerged from the reviewed paths. Review was read-only: one compiler-only stdin probe confirmed finding 1; no tests, edits or agents.
